# Collation Matrix

The utf8mb4 collations on every supported database server: which ones each server has, which one a new table gets, how fast each one is, what converting a table costs, what fails between two collations, and which values compare equal. Generated by the "Collation Matrix" workflow (.github/workflows/collation-matrix.yml); regenerate with:

    gh workflow run collation-matrix.yml   # wait for the run to finish, then:
    gh run download --dir probes           # pick the latest Collation Matrix run
    php .github/scripts/collation-probe.php --merge probes/*/collation-probe-*.json > docs/internal/collation-matrix.md

Last generated: 2026-11-17 from 19 servers, 6 run(s) of each. Generated file - don't hand-edit. The "Verdicts" and "Findings" sections below are maintained in the heredoc in .github/scripts/collation-probe.php; edit them there.

Servers: mysql:5.7 (5.7.44), mysql:8.0 (8.0.46), mysql:8.4 (8.4.13), mysql:9.6 (9.6.0), mysql:9.7 (9.7.4), mariadb:10.2 (10.2.44-MariaDB-1:10.2.44+maria~bionic), mariadb:10.2.6 (10.2.6-MariaDB-10.2.6+maria~jessie), mariadb:10.2.7 (10.2.7-MariaDB-10.2.7+maria~jessie), mariadb:10.3 (10.3.39-MariaDB-1:10.3.39+maria~ubu2004), mariadb:10.4 (10.4.34-MariaDB-1:10.4.34+maria~ubu2004), mariadb:10.5 (10.5.29-MariaDB-ubu2004), mariadb:10.6 (10.6.28-MariaDB-ubu2204), mariadb:10.11 (10.11.19-MariaDB-ubu2204), mariadb:11.4 (11.4.13-MariaDB-ubu2404), mariadb:11.8 (11.8.9-MariaDB-ubu2404), mariadb:12.3 (12.3.3-MariaDB-ubu2404), percona/percona-server:5.7 (5.7.44-48), percona/percona-server:8.0 (8.0.46-37), percona/percona-server:8.4 (8.4.11-12)

## Fastest collation at 1,000,000 rows

Milliseconds per statement on a 1,000,000-row table, one table per collation with the same rows. An empty cell means the server doesn't have that collation. "Fastest" names the quickest one and any other within 5% of it, which is about how far two identical tables differ on these runners. The server's own default is marked with (default). How the test is set up, every other statement and the smaller table sizes are under "Speed" further down.

## Verdicts

CMS Builder 3.84 created every table with `COLLATE=utf8mb4_unicode_ci`. Later versions write `CHARSET=utf8mb4` with no COLLATE, so a new table gets the server's default, an upgraded site has tables in two collations, and a UNION or JOIN across them fails with error 1271 or 1267. Three ways back to one collation, on every server in the matrix (MySQL 5.7 to 9.7, MariaDB 10.2.6 to 12.3, Percona Server 5.7 to 8.4):

| Option | What it costs | What it risks |
|---|---|---|
| A. Every table in the server default; the CMS upgrade converts existing tables | One ALTER per table. Every MySQL and Percona version and MariaDB thru 10.5 copy the table (1.4 to 2.7 s per 100,000 rows here, 21 to 44 s per 1,000,000) and writes wait until it's done. MariaDB 10.6+ rebuilds only the indexes (about 0.3 s per 100,000 rows, 2.6 to 4.4 s per 1,000,000) and writes continue. It is the fastest of the ci collations on MySQL 8.0+ | "The server default" is three different collations, and it changes when the server is upgraded: old tables keep theirs, new tables get the new one, and the two-collation error is back until the convert runs again. Query results change with it (trailing spaces on MySQL 8.0+; ß, æ, ø and emoji everywhere). The convert stops with error 1062 on a UNIQUE column that holds two values the new collation treats as equal. A dump with `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` or `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` doesn't load on an older server or on the other vendor |
| B. Every table in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` | No convert and no change in results for a site that came from 3.84. At 100,000 rows, sorts, GROUP BY, DISTINCT and text joins take 1.3x to 2.1x the default's time on MySQL 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.6, and 1.2x to 2.6x on MySQL 8.0+. A sort of the whole table with no index grows faster than the table does: at 1,000,000 rows and the default `sort_buffer_size` it takes 14.7 s against 0.8 s on MySQL 8.0+, 13.8 s against 2.8 s on MySQL 5.7 and about 8.6 s against 3.3 s on MariaDB thru 10.4 (see the table above). On MariaDB 11.4+ it is up to 14% faster than the default. Point lookups, ORDER BY on an indexed column and INSERT cost the same on every server | Tables created in the server default since the CMS stopped writing COLLATE need the same convert as option A, in the other direction, with the same error 1062 on a UNIQUE column. Any two emoji compare as equal |
| C. New tables copy the collation the site's existing tables have | No convert, no speed change and no change in results | Sites differ from each other: a new install gets the server default, with option A's dump limits. A site that already has two collations still needs one convert and a rule for which collation stays. A table whose default was changed without its columns (`ALTER TABLE t CHARACTER SET utf8mb4`) reports one collation and holds another |

`utf8mb4_unicode_ci` is the only one of the three that exists on all 19 servers under one name and stays the same through a server upgrade. Its price is the sort and grouping times in the speed tables below.

## Findings

Every run gave the same answer for every check: three runs at 100,000 rows and one each at 1,000, 10,000 and 1,000,000. A finding is the same on all 19 servers unless it names versions.

What a new table gets:

- `CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4` with no COLLATE gives `utf8mb4_general_ci` on MySQL and Percona 5.7 and MariaDB 10.2.6 to 10.11, `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` on MySQL and Percona 8.0 to 9.7, and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` on MariaDB 11.4, 11.8 and 12.3. `ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4` with no COLLATE gives the same collation, from a latin1 table and from a `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` table
- MariaDB 11.4 gets its answer from the image's config file, which has the line `character-set-collations = utf8mb4=uca1400_ai_ci`. The built-in default changed in MariaDB 11.5 ([MDEV-25829](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-25829)), so an 11.4 server without that line gives `utf8mb4_general_ci`. The 11.8 and 12.3 images have no such line and give `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`
- The database's default collation, `collation_server` and `SET NAMES ... COLLATE` don't change what a `CHARSET=utf8mb4` table gets. They only apply to a CREATE TABLE with no CHARSET. Two settings do change it: `default_collation_for_utf8mb4` on MySQL 8.0+ and `character_set_collations` on MariaDB 11.4+
- `SHOW CHARACTER SET LIKE 'utf8mb4'`, column `Default collation`, named the collation a new table gets on every server in all seven states tried. `information_schema.CHARACTER_SETS.DEFAULT_COLLATE_NAME` and `COLLATION(CONVERT('a' USING utf8mb4))` are wrong on MySQL 8.0+ once `default_collation_for_utf8mb4` is changed. `information_schema.COLLATIONS ... IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes'` returns no row on MariaDB 11.4+. `@@collation_server`, `@@collation_database` and `@@collation_connection` are each wrong in at least two states
- `utf8mb4_bin`, `utf8mb4_general_ci`, `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci` exist on all 19 servers, and none prints a warning. `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` exists on MySQL and Percona 8.0+ and on MariaDB 11.4+. `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` exists on MariaDB 10.11+ and on no MySQL. A server that doesn't have one answers `error 1273: Unknown collation: 'utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci'`
- A server upgrade keeps every existing column's collation and changes what a new table gets. The upgrade job started MySQL 8.0 on a MySQL 5.7 data directory and MariaDB 11.8 on a MariaDB 10.11 one: the old tables stayed `utf8mb4_general_ci`, a new `CHARSET=utf8mb4` table got `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`, and a UNION of an old and a new table failed with error 1271. The database default stayed `utf8mb4_general_ci`, so a CREATE TABLE with no CHARSET, and a column added to an old table, still got `utf8mb4_general_ci`. `CHECK TABLE ... FOR UPGRADE` reported nothing to rebuild

Speed at 1,000,000 rows, one run (the tables at the top):

- The fastest ci collation is `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` on MySQL and Percona 8.0+ and `utf8mb4_general_ci` on MySQL and Percona 5.7 and on every MariaDB version. That is the server default everywhere except MariaDB 11.4+, where the default `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` takes longer than `utf8mb4_general_ci`: 6% on the whole-table sort, 19% on the first list page, 15% on GROUP BY, and 2.5x on `content LIKE '%word%'` (2,158 ms against 864 ms)
- Sorting every row by a column with no index, which is the sort the last page of a list ordered by `title` needs: `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` takes 14.7 s against 0.8 s for `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` on MySQL and Percona 8.0+ (18x; it was 2.6x at 100,000 rows), 13.8 s against 2.8 s for `utf8mb4_general_ci` on MySQL and Percona 5.7 (4.9x; it was 1.36x), and 7.9 to 9.5 s against 2.2 to 3.7 s on MariaDB 10.2 to 10.4. MariaDB 10.5 and 10.6 are 1.25x and 1.31x, MariaDB 10.11 is 1.11x, and on MariaDB 11.4+ `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` is within 5% of the default
- Those whole-table sorts ran with each server's default `sort_buffer_size`, which 1,000,000 titles don't fit in, so the server sorts in temp files. The ratios are for default settings, not a fixed cost of `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`. The likely reason the difference grows with the table on MySQL and on MariaDB thru 10.4: the older collations sort on a value padded to the column's full width, and write more to those files. Not tested: no run was done with a larger `sort_buffer_size`
- The first list page (`LIMIT 25`, no index on `title`) keeps the ratios it had at 100,000 rows: `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` takes 1.46x the default's time on MySQL 5.7 (691 ms against 472 ms), 1.35x on MySQL 8.0+ (837 ms against 597 ms), 0.97x on MariaDB thru 10.6, 1.12x on MariaDB 10.11 and 0.91x on MariaDB 11.4+. The same page sorted by an integer column takes 225 to 285 ms (470 ms on MySQL 8.0+). With an index on the ORDER BY column it takes 0.1 to 0.3 ms in every collation
- On MariaDB 10.3 to 10.6 the first list page was 3% to 24% faster in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` than in `utf8mb4_general_ci` in this run. At 100,000 rows the two were the same there (0.99x over three runs)
- GROUP BY, SELECT DISTINCT, COUNT(DISTINCT), a join on an indexed text column and ADD INDEX: on MySQL and Percona 8.0+ `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` is within 3% of the default. On MySQL and Percona 5.7 it takes 1.43x to 1.74x the default's time, and on MariaDB thru 10.6 1.10x to 1.93x

Speed at 100,000 rows, three runs:

- A statement that reads rows through an index costs the same in every collation: a point lookup, ORDER BY an indexed column with a LIMIT, and the list page with an index on its ORDER BY column (0.2 ms at every table size). So do `WHERE title = ?` over the whole table and INSERT into a table with a plain index. No collation differs from another there by more than the control does (0.94x to 1.06x)
- `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` against `utf8mb4_general_ci` on MySQL and Percona 5.7 and on MariaDB 10.2 to 10.6, where `utf8mb4_general_ci` is the default: ORDER BY with no index 1.36x and 1.30x, GROUP BY 1.42x and 1.29x, COUNT(DISTINCT) 1.60x and 2.11x, a join on an indexed text column 1.44x and 1.66x, ALTER TABLE ... ADD INDEX 1.38x and 1.68x, the searchMultipleTables() query 1.12x. On MariaDB 10.11 the same statements are 1.05x to 1.20x
- `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` against `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` on MySQL and Percona 8.0+: ORDER BY with no index 2.60x (221 ms against 84 ms), GROUP BY 1.24x, SELECT DISTINCT 1.26x, a join with no index 1.55x, the searchMultipleTables() query 1.29x, INSERT into a UNIQUE text index 1.60x. A join on an indexed text column, COUNT(DISTINCT) and ADD INDEX are within 3%. `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` also sorts faster than `utf8mb4_general_ci` there (0.60x)
- `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` against `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` on MariaDB 11.4+: 0.86x to 1.00x on every statement, so the default is the slower one. `utf8mb4_general_ci` is the fastest ci collation on every MariaDB version: `content LIKE '%word%'` takes 146 ms in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and 337 ms in each of the other four on MariaDB 11.4+
- `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci` costs the same as `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on every server (within 3% on most statements)
- `utf8mb4_bin` is not always the floor: ORDER BY with no index is 1.2x to 1.35x the `utf8mb4_general_ci` time on MySQL and on MariaDB thru 10.6
- Table and index size on disk is the same for all six collations: 83.6 MB of data and 10.1 MB of indexes

The list page, `SELECT * FROM t WHERE hidden = 0 ORDER BY title LIMIT 25` with no index on `title`, at 1,000, 10,000 and 100,000 rows:

- The same query sorted by an integer or a datetime column is the part no collation touches: 0.3 to 0.7 ms at 1,000 rows, 1.2 to 4.7 ms at 10,000 and 25 ms at 100,000 (60 ms on MySQL 8.0+). It is the same in every collation's table
- Sorting by `title` adds to that, at 100,000 rows: 20 ms in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and 43 ms in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on MySQL 5.7; 13 ms in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and 43 ms in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on MySQL 8.0+; about 30 ms in either on MariaDB thru 10.6; 27 ms in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and 31 ms in `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` on MariaDB 11.4+
- So the text sort is 43% of the whole query in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and 62% in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on MySQL 5.7, 18% in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and 42% in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on MySQL 8.0+, and a little over half in every collation on MariaDB
- `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` against the server default, whole query: MySQL 5.7 0.94 ms against 0.67 ms at 1,000 rows, 5.5 against 3.4 at 10,000, 69 against 46 at 100,000. MySQL 8.0+ 0.94 against 0.75, 7.5 against 5.4, 103 against 74. That is 0.2 to 0.3 ms more for each 1,000 rows in the table, in a straight line. MariaDB thru 10.6 shows no difference at any size, and on MariaDB 11.4+ `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` is the faster one by 6 ms at 100,000 rows

Converting a table:

- `ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x` on 100,000 rows (84 MB, six text columns, two text indexes) takes 1.5 to 2.7 s on MySQL and Percona (all versions) and 1.4 to 2.3 s on MariaDB thru 10.5. Those servers copy the table: 100,000 rows affected, a SELECT from another connection runs meanwhile, and an INSERT waits until the ALTER is done. MariaDB 10.6+ takes 0.25 to 0.36 s, reports 0 rows affected and doesn't block the INSERT. Both directions cost the same
- On 1,000,000 rows (834 MB) the same convert takes 24 to 44 s on MySQL and Percona and 21 to 44 s on MariaDB thru 10.5, and an INSERT from another connection waits that long. On the servers that took over 30 s the probe's INSERT stopped at its own lock wait limit with error 1205. MariaDB 10.6+ takes 2.6 to 4.4 s and doesn't block the INSERT
- The form CMS Builder writes, `ALTER TABLE t DEFAULT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x, MODIFY a ... COLLATE x, MODIFY b ... COLLATE x`, takes the same time and uses the same algorithm as CONVERT TO on every server, and leaves the table default and each listed column on x. A `utf8mb4_bin` column it doesn't list stays `utf8mb4_bin`; CONVERT TO changes that column too, and a latin1 column
- After the MODIFY form, MySQL and Percona 8.0+ print `CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x` on each modified column in SHOW CREATE TABLE, even when x is the table default. After CONVERT TO they print none. MariaDB 10.3+ prints none in both cases. MySQL 5.7 and MariaDB 10.2 print a column's COLLATE whenever it isn't `utf8mb4_general_ci`, however the table was made
- A column with no index changes collation without a table copy on MySQL and Percona 8.0+ (`ALGORITHM=INPLACE` works, 0 rows affected) and on MariaDB 10.4+ (`ALGORITHM=INSTANT` works for CONVERT TO, `ALGORITHM=NOCOPY` for the MODIFY form). MySQL and Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.3 copy the table
- An indexed column: `ALGORITHM=INPLACE` is `error 1846: ALGORITHM=INPLACE is not supported. Reason: Cannot change column type INPLACE. Try ALGORITHM=COPY.` on every MySQL and Percona version and on MariaDB thru 10.5 (10.4 and 10.5 give the reason as "Collation change on an indexed column"). It works on MariaDB 10.6+. `ALGORITHM=INPLACE, LOCK=NONE` on a column with no index is error 1845 on MySQL 8.4 and 9.7 and works on MySQL 8.0 and 9.6. `ALGORITHM=COPY, LOCK=NONE` works on MariaDB 11.4+ only
- `ALTER TABLE t CHARACTER SET utf8mb4`, with no CONVERT and no MODIFY, changes the table default only. The existing columns keep `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, SHOW CREATE TABLE then prints `CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on each of them, and a column added afterwards gets the new default. That is two collations inside one table
- A text column in a foreign key stops the convert of either table: error 1832 for the child and 1833 for the parent, and error 3780 for both on MySQL and Percona 8.0+. With `foreign_key_checks = 0` both tables convert on MySQL and Percona and on MariaDB thru 10.3, and the key is still enforced afterwards. MariaDB 10.4+ refuses even then
- A STORED or indexed VIRTUAL generated column, a FULLTEXT index and prefix indexes convert without error, and the prefix lengths stay as they were

Two collations in one statement:

- Between any two ci collations these fail: UNION and UNION ALL (`error 1271: Illegal mix of collations for operation 'UNION'`), `JOIN ... ON a.name = b.name`, `USING (name)`, `IN (subquery)`, `= (subquery)`, `<` and LIKE between the two columns (`error 1267: Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_unicode_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_general_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '='`), and `'text' IN (a.name, b.name)` (error 1270)
- These work: a comparison with a string literal or a prepared-statement parameter, and `INSERT ... SELECT` from one table into the other. CONCAT, CONCAT_WS, COALESCE, IFNULL, IF and CASE over both columns work and return `utf8mb4_bin`. GREATEST does the same, except on MySQL and Percona 5.7 and MariaDB 10.2, where it is error 1267
- A ci column against a `utf8mb4_bin` column works everywhere and compares as `utf8mb4_bin`
- A latin1 column against a utf8mb4 column works with no fix: the server converts the latin1 side
- `col COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci` fixes the UNION, the JOIN and the searchMultipleTables() query between utf8mb4 columns on all 19 servers, written on both sides or only on the side that isn't `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`. On a latin1 column it is `error 1253: COLLATION 'utf8mb4_unicode_ci' is not valid for CHARACTER SET 'latin1'`
- `CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4)` on both sides fixes all three, latin1 columns included, and the result has the server default collation. Written on one side only it fails on most servers. `CAST(col AS CHAR)` on both sides behaves like CONVERT on both sides
- `CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on both sides works in every case tried, latin1 included, and the result is `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on every server
- A function or a COLLATE around a column stops the server from looking that column up in its index: `WHERE name = 'x'` is `type=ref`, and with CONVERT, CAST or `name COLLATE x` it is `type=index`, a scan of the whole index. MySQL and Percona 8.0+ still use the index when x is the column's own collation; no other server does. A COLLATE on the value keeps the index when it names the column's collation and loses it when it names another. ORDER BY with a COLLATE on the column adds a filesort
- In a join, `ON u.name = g.name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci` keeps the index lookup on `u`, the `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` table, when `g` is read first. CONVERT on both sides, or `COLLATE utf8mb4_bin` on both sides, leaves neither table an index lookup
- On the searchMultipleTables() query, `COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci` after every column makes a table in any collation take the time a `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` table takes without it. `CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4)` around every column takes 6% to 17% longer than a table in the server default without it

Results that change:

- Trailing spaces: `'abc' = 'abc '` is true in `utf8mb4_bin`, `utf8mb4_general_ci`, `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`, and false in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`, on MySQL and on MariaDB 11.4+. A UNIQUE index stores both values only in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`. LIKE doesn't match them in any collation
- Emoji: any two emoji are equal in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, and a UNIQUE index refuses the second one with error 1062. They are different in `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci`, `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`
- `'ß' = 'ss'` is false in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and true in the other four ci collations. `'ß' = 's'`, `'i' = 'ı'` and `'I' = 'ı'` are true only in `utf8mb4_general_ci`
- `'æ' = 'ae'`, `'ø' = 'o'`, `'ł' = 'l'`, `'đ' = 'd'` and `'ð' = 'd'` are false in `utf8mb4_general_ci` and `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and true in the three newer ones
- `'é'` against `'e'` plus a combining accent, hiragana against katakana, a fullwidth `'Ａ'` against `'A'`, `'ﬁ'` against `'fi'` and a zero width space inside a word: different in `utf8mb4_general_ci`, equal in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and later. A soft hyphen inside a word is ignored only in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`
- `'a' = 'A'`, `'cafe' = 'café'`, `'ä' = 'a'` and `'ñ' = 'n'` are true in all five ci collations
- So a convert of a UNIQUE column can stop in either direction, with `error 1062: Duplicate entry 'ı' for key 'v'` (MySQL and Percona 8.0+ print the key as `'table.v'`; MariaDB 10.11+ prints an emoji as `?`). Of the 39 sample pairs, 4 are different in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and equal in `utf8mb4_general_ci`, 9 the other way round; 6 are different in `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` and equal in `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`, 7 the other way round, among them `'abc'` against `'abc '` and every emoji pair; 6 and 5 for `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`
- `ALTER IGNORE TABLE ... CONVERT` on MariaDB doesn't stop: it deletes the rows that became duplicates (16 rows became 7 in one check). MySQL and Percona have no ALTER IGNORE (error 1064)
- ORDER BY changes as well. `utf8mb4_general_ci` sorts `_a`, `æ`, `ø` and `ł` after `z`; `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` sorts `_a` first and the letters beside `a`, `o` and `l`. `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` sort emoji before the digits; the older three sort them last

Looked up, not tested:

- No utf8mb4 collation is listed as deprecated in the MySQL 8.0, 8.4 or 9.7 manuals ([8.4 What Is New](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/mysql-nutshell.html)), and no deprecation notice was found for one in MariaDB's documentation. No server in the matrix printed a warning for any of the six
- `default_collation_for_utf8mb4` accepts `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` and `utf8mb4_general_ci` and "is for internal use by MySQL Replication only" ([manual](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/server-system-variables.html#sysvar_default_collation_for_utf8mb4)). Setting it prints a deprecation warning ([bug 104066](https://bugs.mysql.com/bug.php?id=104066))
- MySQL's default changed from `utf8mb4_general_ci` to `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` in 8.0.1 ([release notes](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/8.0/en/news-8-0-1.html)). MariaDB added the uca1400 collations in 10.10 ([MDEV-27009](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-27009)) and `character_set_collations` in 11.2 ([MDEV-30164](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-30164)), and gave that variable its `utf8mb4=utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` default in 11.5 ([MDEV-25829](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-25829))
- MariaDB's `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` is an alias for `utf8mb4_uca1400_nopad_ai_ci`, added in 11.4.5 so that MySQL dumps load ([MDEV-20912](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-20912)). An 11.4 server older than 11.4.5 doesn't have it
- "The upgrade from 5.7 to 8.0 does not change any collation for any existing database objects, but takes effect for new objects" ([MySQL manual](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.0/en/upgrading-from-previous-series.html)), which matches the upgrade job. MariaDB's list of collations that need an index rebuild after an upgrade has no utf8mb4 entry (`collation_changed_order()` in `sql_string.h`)
- Pad attribute: "The Unicode collations based on UCA 9.0.0 and higher have a pad attribute of NO PAD" and the rest are PAD SPACE ([MySQL manual](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/charset-binary-collations.html)). MariaDB names its NO PAD collations with `_nopad_`, so `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` is PAD SPACE, which matches the trailing-space check
- `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` follows Unicode 4.0.0, `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci` 5.2.0, `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` 9.0.0 and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` 14.0.0. For the first, "all supplementary characters are equal to each other" ([MySQL manual](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/charset-unicode-sets.html)), which is the emoji result above
- MariaDB has an open issue that a table can end up with old and new columns in different collations after the default change ([MDEV-40343](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-40343)), and a documentation task that says some comparisons are "significantly slower with utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci" ([MDEV-38743](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-38743)). A sort speed fix for the UCA collations is in 11.8.6 and 12.2.1 ([MDEV-37849](https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-37849))
- Other projects: WordPress creates tables in `utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci` when the server has it and `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` otherwise (`determine_charset()` in `class-wpdb.php`). Laravel 12 and Joomla 5 use `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` on every server; Laravel 11.0.0 shipped with the two server defaults and went back in 11.0.7. Drupal 11 writes no COLLATE unless one is set in `settings.php`
- Amazon RDS, Aurora, Azure and Google Cloud SQL: no page was found that says they change the default utf8mb4 collation. Not known for cPanel hosts

## Server defaults

| Server | A new CHARSET=utf8mb4 table gets | bin | general_ci | unicode_ci | unicode_520_ci | 0900_ai_ci | uca1400_ai_ci |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| mysql:5.7 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mysql:8.0 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |
| mysql:8.4 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |
| mysql:9.6 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |
| mysql:9.7 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |
| mariadb:10.2 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.2.6 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.2.7 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.3 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.4 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.5 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.6 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| mariadb:10.11 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | yes |
| mariadb:11.4 | utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| mariadb:11.8 | utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| mariadb:12.3 | utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| percona/percona-server:5.7 | utf8mb4_general_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | no | no |
| percona/percona-server:8.0 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |
| percona/percona-server:8.4 | utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | no |

## 1. What each server does by default

**1.01 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_bin**

- all servers: accepted

**1.02 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_general_ci**

- all servers: accepted

**1.03 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_unicode_ci**

- all servers: accepted

**1.04 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_unicode_520_ci**

- all servers: accepted

**1.05 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: error 1273: Unknown collation: 'utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci'
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+ and MariaDB 11.4+: accepted

**1.06 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci**

- all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.6: error 1273: Unknown collation: 'utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci'
- MariaDB 10.11+: accepted

**1.07 Settings as the image starts**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.4: character_set_server = latin1; collation_server = latin1_swedish_ci; character_set_collations: no such variable; default_collation_for_utf8mb4: no such variable
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: character_set_server = utf8mb4; collation_server = utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci; character_set_collations: no such variable; default_collation_for_utf8mb4 = utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 10.5, MariaDB 10.6, MariaDB 10.11: character_set_server = utf8mb4; collation_server = utf8mb4_general_ci; character_set_collations: no such variable; default_collation_for_utf8mb4: no such variable
- MariaDB 11.4: character_set_server = utf8mb4; collation_server = utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci; character_set_collations = utf8mb4=utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci; default_collation_for_utf8mb4: no such variable
- MariaDB 11.8+: character_set_server = utf8mb4; collation_server = utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci; character_set_collations = utf8mb3=utf8mb3_uca1400_ai_ci,ucs2=ucs2_uca1400_ai_ci,utf8mb4=utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci,utf16=utf16_uca1400_ai_ci,utf32=utf32_uca1400_ai_ci; default_collation_for_utf8mb4: no such variable

**1.08 Charset and collation lines in the image's config files**

- MySQL 5.7, MySQL 8.0, MySQL 8.4, MySQL 9.6, MySQL 9.7, MariaDB 10.2, MariaDB 10.2.6, MariaDB 10.2.7, MariaDB 10.3, MariaDB 10.4, MariaDB 11.8, MariaDB 12.3, Percona 5.7, Percona 8.0, Percona 8.4: none
- MariaDB 10.5, MariaDB 10.6, MariaDB 10.11: character-set-server  = utf8mb4; collation-server      = utf8mb4_general_ci
- MariaDB 11.4: character-set-collations = utf8mb4=uca1400_ai_ci; character-set-server     = utf8mb4

**1.09 CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 with no COLLATE: the collation the table gets**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: utf8mb4_general_ci
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

**1.10 CREATE TABLE with no CHARSET, in a database created with CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 and no COLLATE**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: utf8mb4_general_ci
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

**1.11 A column declared VARCHAR(10) CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 with no COLLATE, in a utf8mb4_unicode_ci table**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: the column with no charset gets utf8mb4_unicode_ci, the column with CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 gets utf8mb4_general_ci
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: the column with no charset gets utf8mb4_unicode_ci, the column with CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 gets utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: the column with no charset gets utf8mb4_unicode_ci, the column with CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 gets utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

**1.12 ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 with no COLLATE, on a latin1 table**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: utf8mb4_general_ci
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

**1.13 ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 with no COLLATE, on a utf8mb4_unicode_ci table**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11: utf8mb4_general_ci
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

**1.14 Which query returns the collation a new CHARSET=utf8mb4 table gets**

| | as the image starts | database default is utf8mb4_unicode_ci | database default is latin1 | SET SESSION collation_server = utf8mb4_unicode_ci | SET NAMES utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci | SET SESSION default_collation_for_utf8mb4 = utf8mb4_general_ci (MySQL 8.0+) | SET SESSION character_set_collations = 'utf8mb4=utf8mb4_unicode_ci' (MariaDB 11.2+) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CREATE TABLE ... CHARSET=utf8mb4 gets | server default | server default | server default | server default | server default | general_ci (6) | unicode_ci (3) |
| CREATE TABLE with no CHARSET gets | server default | unicode_ci | latin1_swedish_ci | server default | server default | server default (6) | server default (3) |
| CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 on a utf8mb4_unicode_ci table gives | server default | server default | server default | server default | server default | general_ci (6) | unicode_ci (3) |
| CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 on a latin1 table gives | server default | server default | server default | server default | server default | general_ci (6) | unicode_ci (3) |
| SELECT DEFAULT_COLLATE_NAME FROM information_schema.CHARACTER_SETS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' | right | right | right | right | right | wrong (6) | right (3) |
| SHOW CHARACTER SET LIKE 'utf8mb4' (the `Default collation` column) | right | right | right | right | right | right (6) | right (3) |
| SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' | differs [1] | differs [2] | differs [3] | differs [4] | differs [5] | wrong (6) | right (3) |
| SELECT COLLATION(CONVERT('a' USING utf8mb4)) | right | right | right | right | right | wrong (6) | right (3) |
| SELECT @@default_collation_for_utf8mb4 | differs [6] | differs [7] | differs [8] | differs [9] | differs [10] | right (6) | error 1193 (3) |
| SELECT @@character_set_collations (the utf8mb4 entry) | differs [11] | differs [12] | differs [13] | differs [14] | differs [15] | error 1193 (6) | right (3) |
| SELECT @@collation_server | differs [16] | differs [17] | differs [18] | wrong | differs [19] | wrong (6) | wrong (3) |
| SELECT @@collation_database | right | wrong | wrong | right | right | wrong (6) | wrong (3) |
| SELECT @@collation_connection | right | right | right | right | wrong | wrong (6) | wrong (3) |

- [1] SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' / as the image starts: right on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.11; wrong on MariaDB 11.4+
- [2] SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' / database default is utf8mb4_unicode_ci: right on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.11; wrong on MariaDB 11.4+
- [3] SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' / database default is latin1: right on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.11; wrong on MariaDB 11.4+
- [4] SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' / SET SESSION collation_server = utf8mb4_unicode_ci: right on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.11; wrong on MariaDB 11.4+
- [5] SELECT COLLATION_NAME FROM information_schema.COLLATIONS WHERE CHARACTER_SET_NAME = 'utf8mb4' AND IS_DEFAULT = 'Yes' / SET NAMES utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci: right on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 10.11; wrong on MariaDB 11.4+
- [6] SELECT @@default_collation_for_utf8mb4 / as the image starts: error 1193 on MySQL/Percona 5.7 and all MariaDB; right on MySQL/Percona 8.0+

- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: CREATE TABLE `alg` ( `num` int unsigned NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT, `title` varchar(255) NOT NULL, `content` mediumtext NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (`num`) ) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
- MariaDB 10.3, MariaDB 10.4, MariaDB 10.5, MariaDB 10.6, MariaDB 10.11: CREATE TABLE `alg` ( `num` int(10) unsigned NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT, `title` varchar(255) NOT NULL, `content` mediumtext NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (`num`) ) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_general_ci
- MariaDB 11.4+: CREATE TABLE `alg` ( `num` int(10) unsigned NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT, `title` varchar(255) NOT NULL, `content` mediumtext NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (`num`) ) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci

## 4. Two collations in one statement

The column headings name the two collations. "ok, unicode_ci" means the statement ran and its result has that collation. A number in brackets after an answer is how many servers have both collations.

**4.01 Statements that use a column from each of two tables with different collations**

| | unicode_ci and general_ci | unicode_ci and 0900_ai_ci | unicode_ci and uca1400_ai_ci | unicode_ci and unicode_520_ci | general_ci and 0900_ai_ci | general_ci and uca1400_ai_ci | unicode_ci and bin |

| UNION | error 1271 | error 1271 (9) | error 1271 (4) | error 1271 | error 1271 (9) | error 1271 (4) | ok, bin |

| col COLLATE utf8mb4_bin on both sides | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok |
| CAST(col AS CHAR) on both sides | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok |
| CAST(col AS CHAR CHARACTER SET utf8mb4) on both sides | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok |
| CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci on both sides | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok | ok (9) | ok (4) | ok |

- [1] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and general_ci: error 1267 on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 11.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+
- [2] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and 0900_ai_ci: error 1267 on MySQL 8.0, MySQL 8.4, MySQL 9.6, MySQL 9.7, MariaDB 11.4, Percona 8.0, Percona 8.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+
- [3] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and uca1400_ai_ci: error 1267 on MariaDB 10.11, MariaDB 11.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+
- [4] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and unicode_520_ci: error 1267 on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 11.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+
- [5] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / general_ci and 0900_ai_ci: error 1267 on MySQL 8.0, MySQL 8.4, MySQL 9.6, MySQL 9.7, MariaDB 11.4, Percona 8.0, Percona 8.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+
- [6] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / general_ci and uca1400_ai_ci: ok on MariaDB 10.11, MariaDB 11.8, MariaDB 12.3; error 1267 on MariaDB 11.4
- [7] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and bin: error 1267 on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 11.4; ok on MariaDB 11.8+

Details:

- Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_unicode_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_general_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '=' (4 cells, first CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and general_ci; MySQL/Percona 5.7 and MariaDB thru 10.11)
- Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_unicode_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '=' (4 cells, first CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and general_ci; MySQL/Percona 8.0+)
- Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_unicode_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '=' (5 cells, first CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / unicode_ci and general_ci; MariaDB 11.4)
- Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_general_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '=' (CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / general_ci and 0900_ai_ci; MySQL/Percona 8.0+)
- Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_general_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci,IMPLICIT) for operation '=' (CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / general_ci and 0900_ai_ci; CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / general_ci and uca1400_ai_ci; MariaDB 11.4)

- [2] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / JOIN with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column: ok on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 11.4; error 1267 on MariaDB 11.8+
- [3] CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / searchMultipleTables() query with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column: ok on all MySQL/Percona and MariaDB thru 11.4; error 1267 on MariaDB 11.8+

Details:

- Illegal mix of collations (latin1_swedish_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci,CAST) for operation 'UNION' (4 cells, first CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / UNION with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column; MariaDB 11.8+)
- Illegal mix of collations (latin1_swedish_ci,IMPLICIT) and (utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci,CAST) for operation '=' (CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / JOIN with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column; CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) on the second side only / JOIN with a column in the server default; MariaDB 11.8+)
- COLLATION 'utf8mb4_unicode_ci' is not valid for CHARACTER SET 'latin1' (6 cells, first col COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci on both sides / UNION with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column; all servers)
- COLLATION 'utf8mb4_general_ci' is not valid for CHARACTER SET 'latin1' (6 cells, first col COLLATE utf8mb4_general_ci on both sides / UNION with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column; all servers)
- COLLATION 'utf8mb4_bin' is not valid for CHARACTER SET 'latin1' (6 cells, first col COLLATE utf8mb4_bin on both sides / UNION with a utf8mb4_unicode_ci column; all servers)

**4.06 EXPLAIN: WHERE name = 'garden house 1' (no fix)**

- all servers: u: type=ref, key=name

**4.07 EXPLAIN: WHERE CONVERT(name USING utf8mb4) = 'garden house 1'**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.08 EXPLAIN: WHERE CAST(name AS CHAR) = 'garden house 1'**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.09 EXPLAIN: WHERE name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci = 'garden house 1' (the column's own collation)**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and all MariaDB: u: type=index, key=name
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: u: type=ref, key=name

**4.10 EXPLAIN: WHERE name COLLATE utf8mb4_general_ci = 'garden house 1' (another collation)**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.11 EXPLAIN: WHERE name = 'garden house 1' COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci (the column's own collation, on the value)**

- all servers: u: type=ref, key=name

**4.12 EXPLAIN: WHERE name = 'garden house 1' COLLATE utf8mb4_general_ci (another collation, on the value)**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.13 EXPLAIN: WHERE name LIKE 'garden h%' (no fix)**

- all servers: u: type=range, key=name

**4.14 EXPLAIN: WHERE name COLLATE utf8mb4_general_ci LIKE 'garden h%'**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.15 EXPLAIN: ORDER BY name LIMIT 20 (no fix)**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name

**4.16 EXPLAIN: ORDER BY name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci LIMIT 20 (the column's own collation)**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name, filesort

**4.17 EXPLAIN: ORDER BY name COLLATE utf8mb4_general_ci LIMIT 20 (another collation)**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name, filesort

**4.18 EXPLAIN: JOIN of two utf8mb4_unicode_ci tables ON u.name = u2.name (no fix), u read first**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name; u2: type=ref, key=name

**4.19 EXPLAIN: JOIN ON u.name = g.name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci, g read first**

- all servers: g: type=index, key=name; u: type=ref, key=name

**4.20 EXPLAIN: JOIN ON u.name = g.name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci, u read first**

- all servers: u: type=index, key=name; g: type=index, key=name, join buffer

**4.21 EXPLAIN: JOIN ON u.name = g.name COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci, the server picks the order**

- all servers: g: type=index, key=name; u: type=ref, key=name

**4.22 EXPLAIN: JOIN ON CONVERT(u.name USING utf8mb4) = CONVERT(g.name USING utf8mb4), g read first**

- all servers: g: type=index, key=name; u: type=index, key=name, join buffer

**4.23 EXPLAIN: JOIN ON u.name COLLATE utf8mb4_bin = g.name COLLATE utf8mb4_bin, g read first**

- all servers: g: type=index, key=name; u: type=index, key=name, join buffer

## 5. Results that change between collations

A number in brackets after an answer is how many servers have that collation.

**5.01 Is left = right**

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 2. Speed, 1,000,000 rows

One InnoDB table per collation with the same 1,000,000 rows, shaped like a CMS Builder section. It has 15 columns: the record number, three DATETIME, two INT, three TINYINT flags, five VARCHAR(255) and one MEDIUMTEXT. `name` and `titleIndexed` have an index; `title` holds the same text as `titleIndexed` and has none. Average length in characters: name 23, title 36, content 438. The text is mostly ASCII words, with about 9% accented Latin words, 2% words in other scripts and 1% emoji. Numbers are milliseconds per statement, the median of 3 passes (3 for the slow statements), after one uncounted pass. The number in brackets is the time divided by the utf8mb4_general_ci time on the same server in the same run. "Control" is a second set of utf8mb4_general_ci tables timed the same way: how far it is from 1.00x is the noise of that runner, and a difference no larger than that isn't a real difference. The servers ran with innodb_buffer_pool_size = 9,216 MB so every table stays in memory, and with the query cache off.

### utf8mb4_unicode_ci against each server's default

The utf8mb4_unicode_ci time, the time of the collation a new table gets on that server, and the first divided by the second. Each number is the median over the group's servers. Above 1.00x, utf8mb4_unicode_ci is slower than the default.

### Converting a 1,000,000-row table

ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x on a copy of the utf8mb4_unicode_ci table, which has two text indexes (`name` and `titleIndexed`). Milliseconds: the median of every timing (3 in each run), then the fastest and slowest. "Rows affected" is what the server reports: the row count when it copied the table. The last two columns are a SELECT and an INSERT sent from a second connection 200 ms after the convert started.

The MODIFY columns are the same change written as ALTER TABLE t DEFAULT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x with a MODIFY ... COLLATE x for each of the six text columns.

### Query plans

EXPLAIN for the utf8mb4_general_ci table, where the statement is a SELECT.

**List page: SELECT * ... WHERE hidden = 0 ORDER BY title LIMIT 25, no index on title**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY dragSortOrder (an integer, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY createdDate (a datetime, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY titleIndexed (the title again, with an index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=titleIndexed, Using where

**ORDER BY title, whole table, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY title LIMIT 20, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY name LIMIT 20, indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=name, Using index

**WHERE name = ?, indexed (point lookup)**

- all MySQL/Percona: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using index
- all MariaDB: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using where; Using index

**WHERE title = ?, no index (scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE title LIKE '%word%' (VARCHAR, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE content LIKE '%word%' (MEDIUMTEXT, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE name LIKE 'prefix%', indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=range, key=name, Using where; Using index

**GROUP BY title**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary; Using filesort
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**SELECT DISTINCT title**

- all servers: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**COUNT(DISTINCT title)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, 

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, both indexed**

- all servers: a: type=index, key=name, Using index | b: type=ref, key=name, Using index

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, no index, 200 rows of a against all of b**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (Block Nested Loop)
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (hash join)
- MariaDB thru 10.11: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)
- MariaDB 11.4+: b: type=ALL, key=none,  | a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)

**UNION of two tables, then WHERE _content LIKE (searchMultipleTables)**

## 2. Speed, 100,000 rows

One InnoDB table per collation with the same 100,000 rows, shaped like a CMS Builder section. It has 15 columns: the record number, three DATETIME, two INT, three TINYINT flags, five VARCHAR(255) and one MEDIUMTEXT. `name` and `titleIndexed` have an index; `title` holds the same text as `titleIndexed` and has none. Average length in characters: name 22, title 36, content 439. The text is mostly ASCII words, with about 9% accented Latin words, 2% words in other scripts and 1% emoji. Numbers are milliseconds per statement, the median of 5 passes (3 for the slow statements), after one uncounted pass, then the median of 3 runs of the workflow, each on its own runner. The number in brackets is the time divided by the utf8mb4_general_ci time on the same server in the same run. "Control" is a second set of utf8mb4_general_ci tables timed the same way: how far it is from 1.00x is the noise of that runner, and a difference no larger than that isn't a real difference. The servers ran with innodb_buffer_pool_size = 3,072 MB so every table stays in memory, and with the query cache off.

### utf8mb4_unicode_ci against each server's default

The utf8mb4_unicode_ci time, the time of the collation a new table gets on that server, and the first divided by the second. Each number is the median over the group's servers. Above 1.00x, utf8mb4_unicode_ci is slower than the default.

### Converting a 100,000-row table

ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x on a copy of the utf8mb4_unicode_ci table, which has two text indexes (`name` and `titleIndexed`). Milliseconds: the median of every timing (3 in each run), then the fastest and slowest. "Rows affected" is what the server reports: the row count when it copied the table. The last two columns are a SELECT and an INSERT sent from a second connection 200 ms after the convert started.

The MODIFY columns are the same change written as ALTER TABLE t DEFAULT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x with a MODIFY ... COLLATE x for each of the six text columns.

### Query plans

EXPLAIN for the utf8mb4_general_ci table, where the statement is a SELECT.

**List page: SELECT * ... WHERE hidden = 0 ORDER BY title LIMIT 25, no index on title**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY dragSortOrder (an integer, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY createdDate (a datetime, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY titleIndexed (the title again, with an index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=titleIndexed, Using where

**ORDER BY title, whole table, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY title LIMIT 20, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY name LIMIT 20, indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=name, Using index

**WHERE name = ?, indexed (point lookup)**

- all MySQL/Percona: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using index
- all MariaDB: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using where; Using index

**WHERE title = ?, no index (scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE title LIKE '%word%' (VARCHAR, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE content LIKE '%word%' (MEDIUMTEXT, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE name LIKE 'prefix%', indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=range, key=name, Using where; Using index

**GROUP BY title**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary; Using filesort
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**SELECT DISTINCT title**

- all servers: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**COUNT(DISTINCT title)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, 

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, both indexed**

- all servers: a: type=index, key=name, Using index | b: type=ref, key=name, Using index

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, no index, 200 rows of a against all of b**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (Block Nested Loop)
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (hash join)
- MariaDB thru 10.11: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)
- MariaDB 11.4+: b: type=ALL, key=none,  | a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)

**UNION of two tables, then WHERE _content LIKE (searchMultipleTables)**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

**the same UNION with CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) around every column**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

**the same UNION with COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci after every column**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

## 2. Speed, 10,000 rows

One InnoDB table per collation with the same 10,000 rows, shaped like a CMS Builder section. It has 15 columns: the record number, three DATETIME, two INT, three TINYINT flags, five VARCHAR(255) and one MEDIUMTEXT. `name` and `titleIndexed` have an index; `title` holds the same text as `titleIndexed` and has none. Average length in characters: name 21, title 36, content 440. The text is mostly ASCII words, with about 9% accented Latin words, 2% words in other scripts and 1% emoji. Numbers are milliseconds per statement, the median of 5 passes (3 for the slow statements), after one uncounted pass. At this table size a SELECT runs up to 10 times in a pass (200 statements at most) and the time is divided by the count. The number in brackets is the time divided by the utf8mb4_general_ci time on the same server in the same run. "Control" is a second set of utf8mb4_general_ci tables timed the same way: how far it is from 1.00x is the noise of that runner, and a difference no larger than that isn't a real difference. The servers ran with innodb_buffer_pool_size = 3,072 MB so every table stays in memory, and with the query cache off.

### utf8mb4_unicode_ci against each server's default

The utf8mb4_unicode_ci time, the time of the collation a new table gets on that server, and the first divided by the second. Each number is the median over the group's servers. Above 1.00x, utf8mb4_unicode_ci is slower than the default.

| Statement | MySQL and Percona 5.7 (general_ci) | MySQL and Percona 8.0 and later (0900_ai_ci) | MariaDB 10.2 to 10.9 (general_ci) | MariaDB 10.10 to 11.3 (general_ci) | MariaDB 11.4 and later (uca1400_ai_ci) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### Converting a 10,000-row table

ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x on a copy of the utf8mb4_unicode_ci table, which has two text indexes (`name` and `titleIndexed`). Milliseconds: the median of every timing (3 in each run), then the fastest and slowest. "Rows affected" is what the server reports: the row count when it copied the table. The last two columns are a SELECT and an INSERT sent from a second connection 200 ms after the convert started.

The MODIFY columns are the same change written as ALTER TABLE t DEFAULT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x with a MODIFY ... COLLATE x for each of the six text columns.

## 2. Speed, 1,000 rows

One InnoDB table per collation with the same 1,000 rows, shaped like a CMS Builder section. It has 15 columns: the record number, three DATETIME, two INT, three TINYINT flags, five VARCHAR(255) and one MEDIUMTEXT. `name` and `titleIndexed` have an index; `title` holds the same text as `titleIndexed` and has none. Average length in characters: name 20, title 36, content 439. The text is mostly ASCII words, with about 9% accented Latin words, 2% words in other scripts and 1% emoji. Numbers are milliseconds per statement, the median of 5 passes (3 for the slow statements), after one uncounted pass. At this table size a SELECT runs up to 100 times in a pass (200 statements at most) and the time is divided by the count. The number in brackets is the time divided by the utf8mb4_general_ci time on the same server in the same run. "Control" is a second set of utf8mb4_general_ci tables timed the same way: how far it is from 1.00x is the noise of that runner, and a difference no larger than that isn't a real difference. The servers ran with innodb_buffer_pool_size = 3,072 MB so every table stays in memory, and with the query cache off.

### utf8mb4_unicode_ci against each server's default

The utf8mb4_unicode_ci time, the time of the collation a new table gets on that server, and the first divided by the second. Each number is the median over the group's servers. Above 1.00x, utf8mb4_unicode_ci is slower than the default.

### Converting a 1,000-row table

ALTER TABLE ... CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x on a copy of the utf8mb4_unicode_ci table, which has two text indexes (`name` and `titleIndexed`). Milliseconds: the median of every timing (3 in each run), then the fastest and slowest. "Rows affected" is what the server reports: the row count when it copied the table. The last two columns are a SELECT and an INSERT sent from a second connection 200 ms after the convert started.

The MODIFY columns are the same change written as ALTER TABLE t DEFAULT CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE x with a MODIFY ... COLLATE x for each of the six text columns.

### Query plans

EXPLAIN for the utf8mb4_general_ci table, where the statement is a SELECT.

**List page: SELECT * ... WHERE hidden = 0 ORDER BY title LIMIT 25, no index on title**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY dragSortOrder (an integer, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY createdDate (a datetime, no index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using filesort

**List page: the same, ORDER BY titleIndexed (the title again, with an index)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=titleIndexed, Using where

**ORDER BY title, whole table, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY title LIMIT 20, no index**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using filesort

**ORDER BY name LIMIT 20, indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=index, key=name, Using index

**WHERE name = ?, indexed (point lookup)**

- all MySQL/Percona: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using index
- all MariaDB: a_general_ci: type=ref, key=name, Using where; Using index

**WHERE title = ?, no index (scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE title LIKE '%word%' (VARCHAR, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE content LIKE '%word%' (MEDIUMTEXT, scan)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where

**WHERE name LIKE 'prefix%', indexed**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=range, key=name, Using where; Using index

**GROUP BY title**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7 and all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary; Using filesort
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**SELECT DISTINCT title**

- all servers: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary

**COUNT(DISTINCT title)**

- all servers: a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, 

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, both indexed**

- MySQL 5.7, MySQL 8.0, MySQL 8.4, MySQL 9.6, MySQL 9.7, MariaDB 10.2.6, MariaDB 10.4, MariaDB 10.5, MariaDB 10.6, MariaDB 10.11, MariaDB 11.4, MariaDB 11.8, MariaDB 12.3, Percona 5.7, Percona 8.0, Percona 8.4: a: type=index, key=name, Using index | b: type=ref, key=name, Using index
- MariaDB 10.2, MariaDB 10.2.7, MariaDB 10.3: b: type=index, key=name, Using index | a: type=ref, key=name, Using index

**JOIN ON a.name = b.name, no index, 200 rows of a against all of b**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (Block Nested Loop)
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (hash join)
- MariaDB thru 10.11: a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where | b: type=ALL, key=none, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)
- MariaDB 11.4+: b: type=ALL, key=none,  | a: type=range, key=PRIMARY, Using where; Using join buffer (flat, BNL join)

**UNION of two tables, then WHERE _content LIKE (searchMultipleTables)**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

**the same UNION with CONVERT(col USING utf8mb4) around every column**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

**the same UNION with COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci after every column**

- MySQL/Percona 5.7: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none,  | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- MySQL/Percona 8.0+: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none,  | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, Using temporary
- all MariaDB: <derived2>: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | a_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | b_general_ci: type=ALL, key=none, Using where | <union2,3>: type=ALL, key=none, 

## Checks that gave different answers in different runs

None: every check gave the same answer in every run.
